To the north there is river called Tiru nadi, to which no other river can 
bear comparison. It is through bathing in this river that lotus-borne 
[Lakshmi] won her place on Lord Vishnu’s breast. To the south is a river 
called Sona nadi, which even the river Narmada9 praises. It was in this 
river that the Ganges, Yamuna and Kaveri rivers bathed and dispelled 
their sorrow.	 (532)

To the west of  that divine mountain flows the river called Punniya nadi, 
with nearby a town called Punniyarrur. By bathing in that esteemed river, 
a king of  that place by the name of  Izhan was freed from the female 
form his sins had brought upon him, and regained his previous form 
and title.10	 (533)

To the north of  the previously mentioned Tiru nadi is the fair Ceyaru 
river, which Murugan summoned by hurling his noble spear11. It was by 
bathing in its holy waters that He who bears the bright spear [Murugan] 
gained the glorious title of  Slayer of  the asuras, and the eminent name 
of  General of  the gods, who feast on ambrosia.	 (534)

Within the temple of  the Lord of  the dance is a tank called Sivaganga12 

tirtham. To daily fix one’s thoughts upon it, is equivalent to bathing in the 
holy Ganges. It is through joyfully bathing there in the month of  Māci 
that countless Rudras came to ride upon the bull, achieving superiority 
over all the other gods.	 (535)

To the east of  that tank is a tank called Chakra tirtham. It is here that 
Vishnu, whose body is dark like the ocean, bathed in his boar incarnation. 
Those who circumambulate its shores, bathe in it and drink from it will 
escape the ocean of  sorrows and gain the two feet of  the Lord.	  (536)



Holy Tanks 153

To one side of  the temple where the Lord dwells, in the direction of  
Agni, is a tank that bears the name of  Him who dwells in the temple 
of  the lotus, Brahma13 tirtham. Those who immerse their bodies in 
that tank will no more be immersed in the ocean of  birth. They will 
be freed from the evil karma accumulated in each of  one thousand 
births.   	 (537)

If, after bathing in this tank, one gives a piece of  gold no larger than 
an atom, the fruit of  it will be greater than that of  giving to the Lord’s 
devotees enough gold and precious jewels to completely fill up the entire 
earth, girt by the roaring ocean. 	 (538)

Those who give one red cow after bathing in this tank, [covered in] 
nectar-filled [blossoms], will ride in a celestial vahana, like a mountain of  
ruddy gold surrounded by powerful white elephants, with Menaka and 
Aramba waving fine yak-tail fans. Arriving at the feet of  the Lord they 
will remain there for one kalpa for each single hair on that cow.  (539)

Those who give a black cow after bathing at dawn in that tank, will ride 
in a celestial vehicle like a great sapphire mountain, studded densely 
with blue sapphires. With the trident-wielding goblin hordes following 
behind singing praises, they will go to dwell at the feet of  Him who 
bears an eye in his forehead.	 (540)

Those who give a white cow will ride in a celestial vahana, thickly 
inlaid with pearls and surrounded by a thousand white bulls. With the 
assembled hosts of  the unsleeping gods singing praises, they will reside 
at the feet of  the Munificent One. Those who donate an umbrella against 
the rain, sandals, or a steed of  unimpeachable quality will take refuge at 
the feet of  Lord Indra.	 (541)

Those who give away a virgin in marriage on its banks will go to 
bhoga bhumi, the golden world where the gods dwell, to the realm of  
Kubera, and to the shining kingdom of  lotus-dwelling Brahma. Having 
experienced all that they desire in those places, they will remain at the 
feet of  Lord Siva, as the gods incline their heads and sing praises.   (542)



154 Arunachala Puranam

Even if  food were given to ten million great tapasvins in one of  the most 
eminent sthalas, it would not equal a single grain of  boiled rice given in 
immortal Kasi. (Similarly) if  food was given in Kasi to countless crores 
of  great tapasvins, it could not compare to a single grain of  boiled rice 
given in the land of  Arunai, (embodiment of) the real.	 (543)

Those who give to Brahmins a piece of  land, be it no larger than a cow’s 
hoof, will dwell proudly in the shadow of  the Lord’s golden feet with 
all their kith and kin. Those who offer a great bull as a gift to the Lord 
will mount a very different bull, and will ride in state into Siva loka, so 
hard to attain, and there experience bliss.	 (544)

Those who provide for the creation of  tree-lined avenues, flower 
gardens, wells, and tanks surrounded by embankments will reach the 
feet of  vajra wielding [Indra]. Those who adorn the temple, smearing 
cow dung, sweeping, and placing lighted lamps will sleep [like Vishnu] 
in the midst of  the ocean [of  milk] with a serpent for a couch.	 (545)

Those who set up booths to provide much needed water on the days 
when joy-giving rain never falls, will dwell in the luxuriant realm on high, 
praised by the gods themselves. Those who furnish fine garments for 
the devotees of  the Lord who wears the skin of  the hollow-trunked 
elephant, will, for each thread of  yarn, dwell at the Lord’s feet for time 
beyond measure.	 (546) 

Those who build a math [in this holy place] will abide in the state of  
deathless liberation for a whole kalpa for each speck of  dust that 
composes that math. As for those who perform powerful Vedic sacrifices 
there according to prescribed rule on behalf  of  those who have died 
through consuming poison or by other means, their whole clan will 
attain liberation.	 (547)

[Once] a crow, when it was pecking at an offering on the altar, struck 
it with its wing, so that it fell into the temple. The Lord was delighted, 
taking it that his temple has thus been glorified, and granted that crow 
the boon of  dwelling in Indra’s heavenly paradise.	 (548)



Holy Tanks 155

[Once] when a rat was digging its burrow in the temple and a rare and 
precious jewel fell down, flashing like a lamp, the Lord of the dance, taking 
it that a holy lamp had been lit in offering, instructed that rat in the lofty 
path that leads to liberation, and swiftly granted it the boon of  dwelling 
with Him in heaven14.	 (549)

One day on that mountain, when a spider had spun some thread and 
woven it [into a web], [the Lord], thinking, ‘Here is some garment that 
has been donated to Us,’ transformed that spider into a virtuous king, 
conferred upon him true knowledge, and, after his death, granted him 
the boon of  dwelling in the kingdom of  tall Mal.	 (550)

One man who shaped a heap of  sand into the form of  Lord Siva in that 
place subsequently gained the supreme state of  liberation and attained 
the condition of  identity with the Lord. This is the incomparable 
excellence of  the holy place, Arunagiri.”15 Having spoken thus, the 
illustrious One who gave forth the Vedas remained in silence, a smile 
illuminating the holy orb of  his lotus-like countenance.’	 (551)



References

1.	 Murugan [Murukaṉ] is an important deity in pre-Aryan Tamil culture who later 
became identified with the Aryan deity Skanda / Subramaṇya / Kārtikeya. He 
is the patron deity of the kuriñci tiṇai – mountain tracts. See v. 295, note 19.

2.	 Here two legendary exploits of Murugan are referred to. In the first, he is 
mentioned as destroying the demon Surapadman, after the latter made himself 
invisible and stood on the bottom of the ocean in the form of a monstrous mango 
tree. In the second, he is mentioned as the slayer of the asura Krauncha who 
took the form of a mountain in order to entrap rishis and others. Krauncha was 
a brother of Surapadman, as was Tarakan, who has been mentioned previously 
as an asura defeated by Murugan. See verses 208 and 255.

3.	 See the Introduction p. xii for a discussion of the sources of the Aruṇācala 
Purāṇam. 

4.	 This story is the subject of chapter 13.

5.	 The day in the month of Tai when the moon is in the asterism (group of stars) 
called pūcam. This is an auspicious time for eating the first rice, bathing, and 
taking offerings to the temple. The month of Tai extends from mid-January to 
mid-February.

6.	 The month of Paṅkuṉi extends from mid-March to mid-April.

7.	 Niruti Sanskrit Nirṛti is the deva who guards the south west direction. See verse 
392. The Purāṇas do not appear to have much to say about him, other than that 
he performs this role.

8.	 aṅgam means limb, and the Vedaṅgas are the branches of science which are 
considered separate from and subsidiary to the Vedas, such as astrology and 
astronomy. 

9.	 The Narmada is one of the holy rivers of India, and forms the traditional 
boundary between north and south India.

10.	 This river and town, and the story relating to it, have not been identified.

11.	 See v. 206ff. in which Murukaṉ splits the ground with his spear to bring forth 
water for Pārvatī’s ritual ablutions.

12.	 The Śivagaṅga tīrtham is one of the two large tanks in the Aruṇācaleśvarar 
temple. It is in the outer, fifth prakāram – avenue, to the left of the eastern 
entrance gate.



Holy Tanks 157

13.	 The Brahmā tīrtham is the tank in which Pārvatī bathes in chapter 5. See v. 387, 
note 22.

14.	 cālōkam, Sanskrit sālokya. This is the lowest of the four states – patavi of bliss; 
the next highest is cāmīpam – nearness to god, then cārūpam – bearing the 
likeness of god, and finally cāyucciyam – identity with god.

15.	 We have taken it that, as implied in v. 515, Nandi has been quoting Brahmā 
directly for the bulk of this chapter (from v. 516 onwards). This remains the 
pattern for the remainder of the Purāṇam, with Nandi relating the conversation 
between Brahmā and Sanaka, rather than telling what he has heard directly from 
Śiva (see v. 8). Alternatively, it may be that the narrator has changed altogether 
for the remainder of the Purāṇam, and is now Brahmā. In the Māhātmya itself, 
the narrator of the first section, the Purvārdha, is Brahmā, who is addressing 
Sanaka, whilst in the second section, the Uttarārdha, it is Nandi, who is 
addressing Markaṇṭeya.






Chapter 9

திருமலை வலம்புரிச் சருக்கம்

Pradakshina of the Holy Mountain






‘The rishi Sanaka arose, made prostrations and said, “Great lotus-borne 
One, hearing of  the glory of  those tanks, girt by golden lotus blossoms, 
has brought me great joy! Pray explain to me now, so that I clearly 
understand, the act of  circumambulating that mountain which no sin 
mars.” In reply [Brahma] the god who dwells in the [lotus] temple, which 
the sun opens and closes with the sceptre of  his rays, spoke these words:
	 (552)

“Even those who have performed the most arduous tapas for many 
kalpas will not be able to perceive its greatness. Will then they whose 
tapas is a mere trifle be able to comprehend the virtue of  pradakshina 
of  Arunagiri? I began this exposition in the awareness that you were 
desirous of  hearing of  the glory of  Lord Siva. May you now feast on 
bliss as I pour the nectar of  this wondrous tale into the conduits of  
your ears!” he said.	 (553)

“Pradakshina of  Sonagiri is the vessel that will carry you across the great 
ocean of  birth; it is the ladder that will permit you to rise up on the 
path of  liberation, not falling into the deep abyss of  the seven hells. It 
confers the title deed to austerities that defy comprehension, to virtue, 
and to riches immeasurable. It confers a joy that will melt the heart of  
Him who bears an eye on his forehead, and shares his form with the 
Maiden with the luminous brow.	 (554)

If  one performs puja to the Lord for an entire kalpa in Kasi and 
other glorious sthalas, practices arduous tapas for long ages, or attains 
supernatural powers by remaining in samadhi in accordance with the 
instructions of  a guru, will the reward gained be equal to merely crying 
out with love, ‘Come, let us perform pradakshina of  Arunagiri!’?	 (555)

The moment the cry goes up, ‘Come, let us perform pradakshina of  
Arunagiri,’ the fundamental sin of  Brahminicide will go away; the sin 
of  drinking as much toddy as there is water in the Narmada river will be 
troubled, shiver with fright and run off; the age-old sin of  plundering 
the vast wealth of  others will join its palms respectfully, take its leave 
and run; even the evil act of  transgression against our guru-Lord will 
be afraid, its whole foundation undermined.	 (556)



162 Arunachala Puranam

The sin of  murder will be snuffed out like a lighted lamp standing in a 
great wind; the sin of  patricide will languish and be reduced to nought, 
like a snake that has seen Garuda. The ocean of  sin that is the killing 
of  women and children will evaporate and dry up. The sin of  stealing 
the goods of  our Father will wonder what refuge now there is for it.
	 (557)

Taking a single step [on pradakshina] will bestow the benefits of  
performing a yaga (Vedic sacrifice). Two steps will have the effect of  a 
raja suya sacrifice1. Three sweet, righteous steps will bring all the blessings 
of  an asva medha2. Four steps upon the earth will bestow the benefit of  
all the yagas combined.	 (558)

Taking a single step in pradakshina will be equivalent to performing 
pradakshina of  the entire world. Lifting a foot and taking two steps will bestow 
the benefit of  bathing in [all] tanks. Taking three steps in that holy place 
will confer the benefits of  giving the sixteen types of  gifts.  Four glorious 
steps will bestow the fruits of  the eight branches of  yoga.	 (559)

A single step taken will confer all the fruits of  [Siva] puja. Two paces 
completed there will confer the powerful fruits of  setting up a [Siva] 
lingam. If  I am to speak of  the benefits of  taking three steps, its reward 
will be equal to building a crore of  vimanas in the pinnacled temple.   (560)

Those who take a single step in pradakshina of  lofty Arunagiri will have 
gained for themselves a blissful life in the realm of  Rudra. Those who 
in devotion take two steps will have attained the dwelling place of  
Maheswara. Those who arise and take three steps in pradakshina around 
the Mountain adorned with writhing serpents and the river Ganges, will 
send ten million generations of  mankind to dwell in Sivaloka.	 (561)

For those who walk a small distance on the pradakshina path of  beneficent 
Arunagiri, the path around silver mount Kailash, which emits dense, 
long rays, will soon fail in comparison. Or, sins will flee as if  they have 
seen Death himself, if  they so much as set eyes on feet that have been 
burned by the searing heat of  the hot pebbles as they walk in pradakshina 
around that mountain.	 (562)



Pradakshina of  the Holy Mountain 163

If  the wind should carry a single speck of  dust3 from the feet of  those 
who have performed pradakshina of  the Fire Mountain into any place, be 
it vile as hell itself, that place will attain the purity of  Mount Kailash. Or, 
if  a speck of  dust from their feet should fall onto the body of  any man, 
death will end for them, and close pursuing birth will disappear. How 
might I express in words the greatness of  those who have performed 
pradakshina?	 (563)

When those who have performed pradakshina of  that mountain proceed 
to Mount Kailash, the moon will be carrying a silver umbrella, and the 
sun, a lighted lamp. The Goddess of  Virtue will take them by the arm. 
Heaven-dwelling Indra will carpet their path with blossoms. The King 
of  Alaka [Kubera] will attend them closely, paying homage. The [eight] 
Vasus4 will pour down a rain of  flowers.	 (564)

Menaka, Aramba, Urvasi and Tilottama will dance and sing, accompanied 
by the veena. The rivers Ganga and Yamuna will wave yak-tail fans. The 
clouds will come to meet them, bearing ambrosia, delicious to eat, 
and vestments as tall as mountains. Lotus-dwelling Lakshmi will be in 
attendance, bearing flowers and fragrant musk. At the fore will march 
the Vedas, singing praises.	 (565)

The fruits of  bathing in the Ganges, which teems with leaping 
carp, visiting renowned holy sthalas, and practising yoga do not bear 
comparison. Do not think that pradakshina of  Sonachala, whose matted 
locks are adorned with atti flowers, is of  limited benefit. Think of  the 
rewards as all-embracing.5	 (566)

For those who perform pradakshina of  this great and noble mountain, 
to which the gods ever pay homage, their feet, which have grown red 
through treading on the pebbles spread across their path, will become 
red once more from the light reflected from the rubies set into the tips 
of  the crowns of  the gods who bow down low before them, in the hope 
of  receiving boons.	 (567)

Sanaka, for those who do not perform pradakshina of  the mountain, what 
is the advantage of  being able to walk?  What is the use of  being able to 



164 Arunachala Puranam

see, with eyes that have been darkened? What is the point of  attaining 
human birth? Inhabiting a worm-infested bag of  flesh, they can only 
tumble into hell, and wander there in torment; there is no salvation for 
them,” said Brahma.	 (568)

“Whether they desire union with maidens with waists like the vanci 
creeper, whether they desire to subject kings to their rule, whether they 
wish, in an instant, to put an end to all the enmity that gives them cause to 
fear, whether they wish to father fine children, fair as the young crescent 
moon, whether they wish to escape sanchita and the other two karmas, 
whether they wish to visit their wrath upon [rakshashas like] Muyalagan, 
evil spirits, or the hordes of bhutas, whether they wish to fend off diseases 
that bring suffering, or the ills of the body, whether they wish to master 
iyal or icai 6 Tamil, whether they desire beauty beyond description, or 
whether they desire glory, free of all taint, the great ones will perform 
pradakshina of that mountain.	 (569 & 570)

Those who perform pradakashina on Sunday, granter of  boons, will 
rend the disk of  the sun and attain the state of  liberation with Siva as 
their Lord.7 Those who perform pradakshina on Monday, bestower of  
strength, will rule the seven worlds like Indra, and share the nature of  
Lord Siva Himself.	 (571)

Those who perform pradakshina on the auspicious day (Tuesday), will be 
released from debt and poverty, and will escape the chains of  the seven 
births that pursue them. Those who circumambulate our Lord on [the 
day of] Mercury (Wednesday) will clearly comprehend the four Vedas, 
their six auxiliary branches and the [sixty-four] great arts and sciences. 
Ripe for liberation, they will dwell as gods.	 (572)

Those who go round the mountain of  ruby on the day of  great Jupiter 
(Thursday), will become preceptors of  the gods and rishis, as the Three 
(Brahma, Vishnu and Siva) sing praises. Those who perform pradakshina 
on the flawless day of  Venus (Friday), will dwell in the realm of  Vishnu, 
he who embraces the lotus-dwelling Lakshmi.	 (573)



Pradakshina of  the Holy Mountain 165

For those who perform pradakshina at dawn on the day of  Saturn 
(Saturday), the nine planets will confer excellent benefits in all sixteen 
areas. The benefits will be even greater on the night of  moon-crowned 
Siva (Sivaratri), the first day of  the new year, and in the months of  Aipaci, 
[Karttikai and Markazhi].	 (574)

For those who perform pradakshina on the solstices, in the month of  
Maci, at the time of  Ardha udaya8, when the serpent [Ketu] swallows 
the sun that gives heat9, or in the Viyatipata yoga10, the power of  these 
benefits will be ten millionfold. Who could ever describe the benefits 
accruing to those who perform pradakshina daily with unflagging energy?
	 (575)

Avoiding sexual union with women that day, even in dream, taking a 
bath, putting on clean clothes, smearing his body with holy ash, giving 
charitable gifts to others, but accepting none himself, walking on foot, 
worshipping and praising the gods who are also performing pradakshina..,
	 (576)

…eschewing any form of  tunic or cloak, neither carrying an umbrella 
nor wearing sandals, leaving aside at this time fear, anger and sorrow, 
not using any form of  transport, such as a horse or elephant, rejecting 
betel and foodstuffs on the way..,	 (577)

…not waving the arms about, but joining them together above the 
head, with an alert mind and a joyful demeanour, averting his gaze from 
women, sinners11, those who are base and vile, and those missing limbs, 
[walking softly] so that his footfalls cannot be heard, walking like the 
wives of  kings in the tenth month of  pregnancy..,	 (578)

…prostrating himself  at the eight cardinal points of  the mountain which 
wipes out good and evil karma, praising and worshipping the gods that 
surround it in those eight directions, mentally offering praises to the 
gods and siddhas who perform pradakshina without physical form, lovingly 
keeping to the (outer) side (of  the path), lest they touch them with their 
hands or feet..,12 	 (579)



166 Arunachala Puranam

…he should walk in silence, or listening to words spoken in praise of  
the Lord, imagining that the mountain, which reaches up to the sky, 
is of  gold, crystal or ruby; performing pradakshina in this manner, he 
should enter the temple, worshipping the feet of  Him whom [Vishnu 
in the form of] the boar could not know, and thus reach the abode [of  
the Lord].	 (580)

Remaining there, surrounded by those [devotees] whose qualities are as 
lofty as mountains, he should consume sweet fruits and milk; or he should 
eat food that contains no meat. This is the course that should be adopted 
by those who dwell in that sthala which stands as (the embodiment of) 
righteousness. For those who have come from a great distance, suffering 
greatly, pradakshina will be beneficial, however they perform it.	 (581)

Sanaka, you who possess indestructible jnana, around this mountain 
the Guardians of  the Eight Directions have set up, each in their own 
name, (lingams that are) the form of  Lord Siva. On the east side of  
the mountain the daughter of  the Himalaya, whose locks resound to 
the hum of  flying beetles, has established upon this hill of  coral hue an 
image of  the Lord of  Mount Kailash [in the form of  a lingam].	  (582)

On the south side Vajrangada Pandiyan, who circled this mountain 
of  ruby on horseback, established [a lingam called] ‘the Lord who is 
perfumed with civet’, to dispel the sin that clung to him at that time. In 
order to remove the evil karma which could not be destroyed, I myself, 
miserable wretch that I am, established on the west side of  the mountain 
[a lingam which symbolises] the Lord both with and without form, by 
the name of  ‘Fair Annamalai.’13	 (583)

In former times the bright, hot sun was greatly afraid, [having committed 
the sin of] travelling over the [top of] Sonagiri14, and established [a lingam 
to] the Lord in his own name. It is impossible to convey how many are 
the emblems of  the Lord that have been set up in all its temples by the 
gods, the inhabitants of  naga loka, siddhas and righteous rishis.   	 (584)

Lingams that are of  the form of  the Lord surround that place to a 
distance of  three leagues, crowding densely like the stars in the heavens. 



Pradakshina of  the Holy Mountain 167

How might I describe the greatness of  that sthala, when it is beyond the 
powers even of  Murugan, bright Gauri or elephant-headed Ganesha to 
do so?”	 (585)

When [Brahma], he who dwells upon the lotus with its spreading 
filaments, had finished speaking, Sanaka made obeisance and said, 
“Lord of  the Vedas! Flawless One who destroys birth! Pray tell of  the 
crimes of  those who disparage the Immaculate One, and of  those who 
purloin his bright gold, and of  the means by which their sins may be 
wiped out,” to which Brahma replied…’	 (586)



References

1.	 irāca sūyam, Sanskrit rāja sūya, is a sacrifice performed by a victorious monarch 
before his vanquished tributary princes.

2.	 pari mētam, Sanskrit aśva medha is the horse sacrifice, in which a king releases 
a horse and attempts to conquer the territories to which it wanders.

3.	 Compare Ar. Mā, Pūr. Ch. 9, sl. 35. 

	 asya pādarajaḥsparśāt pūyate sakalā mahī, padam ekaṁ tu dhatte yaḥ śoṇàdrīśa 
pradakṣiṇe.

	 The entire earth will be blessed by contact with the dust of the feet of one who 
takes but a single step in pradakṣiṇa of Śoṇādri.

4.	 See v. 93, note 30.

5.	 Tamil text: there is a play on the word kāl in line four, which can mean either 
foot or quarter. The line says literally, ‘not thinking it a foot or quarter benefit, 
think it an entire benefit.’

6.	 There are three modes of Tamil: iyal tamiḻ – prose, icai tamiḻ – verse, and nāṭaka 
tamiḻ – dramatic Tamil.

7.	 The text here follows closely that of the Māhātmya in describing the benefits 
conferred by pradakṣiṇā on specific days. For example, for Sunday the 
Māhātmya says:

	 yaḥ karoti naro bhūmau sūryavāre pradakṣiṇāṁ, sa sūryamaṇḍalaṁ bhittvā 
muktaḥ śivapuraṁ vrajet.

	 He who on the earth performs pradakṣiṇā on Sunday will break through the 
disk of the sun, and, liberated, will go to the city of Śiva.

	 Ar. Mā, Pūr. Ch. 9, sl. 57b-58a.

8.	 The arttōtayam, Sanskrit ardha + udaya, is a sacred conjunction of the sun and 
the moon at sunrise on a Sunday.

9.	 In Hindu astrology the serpent refers to the rising and descending nodes. See 
v.89, note 16.

10.	 Vitipātam, Sanskrit Vyatīpāta, is one of 27 yogas, which are derived by dividing 
the combined longitudes of the sun and moon by 13 degrees 20 minutes. Those 
born in this yoga are said to face great problems and setbacks at the outset, but 
to triumph eventually through their determination in the face of adversity.



Pradakshina of  the Holy Mountain 169

11.	 patitar – literally, those who have lost caste or renounced their religion, 
apostates.

12.	 Compare Ar. Mā, Pūr. Ch. 9, sl. 39-40:

	 manūnāṁ caratām agre devānāṁ ca sahasraśaḥ adṛśyānāṁ ca siddhānāṁ 
nānyeṣāṁ vāyurūpiṇāṁ saṅghaṭṭamatisammadaṁ mārgarodhaṁ vicintayan, 
anukūlena bhaktaḥ sañchanair dadyāt padaṁ budhaḥ.

	 Thinking about the likelihood of collision with thousands of devas, invisible 
siddhas and others of immaterial form, as well as Manus, who walk ahead, and 
of the consequent rush and obstruction to the path, the true devotee should go 
ahead slowly, placing his steps.

13.	 The lingam is housed in the temple of Aṭiaṇṇāmalai which is situated in the 
village of the same name on the north west side of the mountain. The word 
aṭi means origin, foundation in Tamil. The temple is reputed to be even more 
ancient than the main Aruṇācalēsvarar temple.

14.	 This story is told in chapter 10.






Chapter 10

ஆதித்தச் சருக்கம்

The Sun






‘ “As for those who revile Arunai’s Mountain, which is surrounded by 
taint-free sages, claiming that some other mountain is its equal, their 
mouths will become cancerous, will exhale smoke and be filled with 
writhing worms. A foul liquid stinking of  rotting flesh will well up and 
pour down in a flood [over their body], along with a heavy downpour 
of  blood.	 (587)

Emissaries sent by the Lord of  Death will come, take them, grind them 
over and over again in an oil-press, then take them out and slash their 
mouths with red hot swords. Crying, ‘Would you insult our father, the 
Red Mountain Lord!’ they will beat them and have them boiled in oil.
	 (588)

Beating them till the fibres fly like cotton, melting wax and pouring it 
into their mouths, forcing poison down their throats, they will consign 
them to the burning pit of  hell. Even that hell will be fearful of  them, 
feel loathing for them and torment them. The same fate will await those 
who merely entertain such thoughts [about Arunachala] in their minds.
	 (589)

When they are consigned to the burning hell where dwell evil people 
such as those who have murdered their guru, father or mother, even 
those wicked ones will be greatly distressed, and call them ‘sinners’, 
saying, ‘It is not right that we should be with such people, get rid of  
them,’ and covering their eyes.”	 (590)

Brahma continued, “Arunachala is the mountain which, from that first 
day till now, neither I, nor Vishnu, who swallowed the seven worlds, 
have been able to fathom. Yet one there was who scorned it as just a 
mountain, and was scorched and brought low, his body wasted.” So 
saying, he went on to tell that tale:	 (591)

“Arunachala is a divine mountain, which, though it stands in the world 
as an ordinary mountain, contains within itself  all the heavenly lights 
that move about (in the heavens). However the Sun with his [thousand] 
rays, bearing a discus, deeming it to be just a mountain like any other, 
drove his bright chariot with seven horses right over the summit.   (592)



174 Arunachala Puranam

But before he even reached the summit of that mountain, whose foot 
Mukunda1 sought, a vast effulgence exploded from within; rising up 
amidst heat and smoke, it burned the chariot to a crisp, just as [Lord 
Siva burned up] the three cites of  the asuras. As for the team of  fresh 
horses, they became dried-up husks.	 (593)

The Sun was distraught; his horses were reduced to charcoal; his banner-
flying chariot became a demon chariot2, and his rays were lost, as if  the 
charioteer had lost his legs. Then I, who perform pradakshina of  the 
mountain to gain the path of  jnana, looked at him and spoke these words:
	 (594)

‘Ignorant fool, you have been brought low, thinking egotistically that 
the Lord is but a mountain! There could never [otherwise] be salvation 
for you. However there is one thing you can do to gain it: perform 
pradakshina of  Arunagiri, who is known as reality itself  to those of  clear 
understanding.’ Having spoken thus I returned from there.	 (595)

Adhering to the instructions given by myself, the Sun, having first circled 
Mount Meru, performed pradakshina of  Sonachala, whereupon He who 
wields an axe to torment his foes appeared – shining like ten million 
suns and mounted upon a young bull – bestowed his grace upon the 
Sun and departed.	 (596)

During each of  the twelve months the Sun goes round that Mountain of  
Fire in pradakshina, such was the fear he experienced at that time.  Never 
does he approach its summit. Never does he circle it with the mountain 
on his left side. His chariot, when it comes near that mountain, sings 
praises, pouring forth a rain of  blossoms as it goes.”	 (597)



References

1.	 Mukunda is one of the names of Viṣṇu.

2.	 alakai-t-tēr – demon chariot, is the Tamil word for a mirage, as seen in the 
desert. The more common form is pēy-tēr with the same meaning.  






Chapter 11

பிரதத்த ராஜன் சருக்கம்

King Pradatta






‘Those who steal so much as an atom of  the wealth of  the Lord who 
dwells in Arunai, will die on the spot, and will be engulfed in the fires 
of  the realm of  Yama. They will be pounded with a diamond pestle. 
They will dwell in twenty eight crores of  black hells, one after the other, 
before being born upon the earth again as worms. Any who conspire 
in the theft will also meet the same fate.  	 (598)

The bodies of  those who come near to [stolen] gold, hidden in the 
broad earth will be chastised by it. Such is the power of  the wealth of  
the Lord of  Arunai, whose grace is abundant. As for those who hide 
it in the earth and later retrieve it, it will slay them along with all their 
kindred. If  they leave it hidden in the mountains or under the ocean, it 
will pursue them. Will it ever let them escape? It will follow them like a 
shadow, wherever they go.	 (599)

We may bite on stone; we may swallow fire; we may mash up deadly 
poisons, mix them together and drink them; we may swallow iron; we 
may drink spurge milk; but we can remove their harmful effects through 
the healing power of  medicine; but if  one consumes the wealth of  the 
Lord of  Arunai, it will grip the throat; it will fill the body with smoke 
and fire.  Will it stop at that? No! It will destroy their mind and their 
very existence.	 (600)

Leaving aside those who have stolen the wealth of  the Three-eyed One, 
those who try to steal, or even think of  stealing his wealth, will die then and 
there. Leaving those aside also, Brahma, he who eclipses even the ruddy glow 
of  the evening sky with the lustre of  his jewelled ornaments, told many a 
true story of  how those who think it right to persecute the devotees of  the 
Lord and steal their goods are destroyed. Then he told this tale:	 (601)

“Once, in former days, there was a king of  the northern land; his kingdom 
was on the shores of  the Ganges whose waters hold priceless pearls; he 
was like Indra himself, come to dwell upon the earth; in manliness he 
resembled Lord Murugan himself; he wore sparkling golden anklets that 
were worn down by the large diamonds set in the points of  the crowns 
of  the kings [who bowed down to him], rank upon rank; he did not stray 
from the path of  dharma. His glorious name was Pradatta.  	 (602)



180 Arunachala Puranam

Shining like the faces of  maidens whose gaze is as deadly as Yama 
himself, like the moon, from whose face the blemish has been entirely 
removed, supported by a pole enchased with blue sapphires, his white 
umbrella cast its shadow over all the wide earth. As his two yak-tail fans 
waved, it was as if  the goddess of  virtue was beckoning welcomingly to 
rebellious kings, saying, ‘If  you submit to his golden feet, your life will 
be spared!’	 (603)

As he mounted a military expedition, horses of  emerald hue shone, 
radiant as the steeds of  Varuna’s realm; a forest of  chariots with banners 
that reached up to the realm of  the sun covered the earth; the rays from 
the ornaments on the tusks of  fine elephants with spotted faces flashed 
in all directions, and [enemy] kings trembled with fear.	 (604)

The kings of  Angadesa1 climbed termite mounds; the Yavana2 kings 
took refuge in the mountains; those of  Avanti3 gripped the strings of  
their bows; with the rest of  the kings trailing after him in a chain, each 
carrying his own tribute, Pradatta, like a male lion, reached the Chola 
land. Crossing the Tiru nadi, that guardian of  Kasi and the river Ganges 
reached southern Arunai and entered the temple of  the Lord who wears 
the holy river in his matted locks.	 605)

Filled with devotion, and bearing flowers whose buds were just opening, 
he entered the holy temple of  the Lord of  Arunai to perform puja. At 
the place where he was performing worship, he heard a temple maiden 
singing, surrounded by her brightly bejewelled companions; her words 
were sweet as pure milk and sugarcane; her gaze was piercing and more 
deadly than poison, and the sound of  her voice could have melted iron. 
The king fell victim to Kama’s arching bow of  sugarcane.	 (606) 

‘Is it a swan? Is it a parrot? Is it divine ambrosia? Is it goddess Lakshmi 
herself ? Is it the beautiful Mohini4 who on that day gave the ambrosia 
to the gods? Is it Rati5, or Menaka6, who was created from the mind 
of  Brahma?’ he cried, and, intoxicated with lust, conceived the idea of  
taking her off  to his own country. Barely had this thought crossed his 
mind when his face was transformed into that of  a monkey, at which a 
smile broke out on the faces of  all.	 (607)



King Pradatta 181

The elders amongst the king’s ministers, having made enquiries 
[concerning the condition] of  the king, said, ‘King, this suffering has 
come about as a result of  your [evil] thoughts. In order to remove it, 
pay homage to the ambrosial One who dwells upon this mountain!’ 
And so the king offered up his elephants, great as mountains, his swift 
horses, his palanquins encrusted with precious gems, his chariots, and 
many maidens with breasts smeared with sandalwood paste, and placed 
them before the gold covered temple.	 (608)

Pujas without number he performed, hard to describe, and so the Lord 
granted him his grace. The distressing monkey face he had acquired 
through his desire for a maiden whose words were sweet as those of  the 
celestial kinnaras, disappeared. Regaining his handsome human features, 
the king returned to his land. Therefore the sin of  planning to steal the 
wealth of  the Lord can be expiated by a gift amounting to ten times the 
value of  that wealth,” concluded Brahma.	 (609)

“Worthy ascetic, this then is the richness of  Arunagiri,” he said, the 
words falling like blossoms from his divine mouth. Worshipping the 
feet of  that divine Brahma, Sanaka said, “We have been delighted by 
your teaching. Now pray recount to us the stories of  how upon this 
earth sins knowingly performed may be expiated.” And Brahma, he of  
mature understanding, uttered words from his four mouths, as if  they 
were a thousandfold.’	 (610)



References

1.	 Aṅkar are the inhabitants of Aṅga Deśa, the country around Benares (Kāci).

2.	 Yavaṉar the people of Yavaṉam; ‘A country variously identified with Ionia, 
Greece, Bactria and more recently Arabia.’ (Tam. Lex.) Presumably people of 
that race who settled in India are meant.

3.	 Avantiyar are the people of Ujjain, also known as Avanti, in Madhya Pradesh. 
It has been mentioned previously as one of the seven holy cities of India.

4.	 Mōkiṉī, Sanskrit Mohinī is Viṣṇu in female form. At the time of the churning 
of the ocean of milk, a quarrel broke out between the devas and asuras over 
the right to eat the ambrosia churned from the ocean. Viṣṇu appeared in female 
form to distract the asuras, whilst the gods made off with the ambrosia.

5.	 Irati, Sanskrit Ratī is the wife of Kāma the god of love.

6.	 Mēṉakai, Sanskrit Menakā, is a beautiful celestial courtesan.



Chapter 12

பாவம் தீர்த்த சருக்கம்

The Expiation of Sins






“Those who have incurred sins through thoughts, words or deeds, if  
they fix their thoughts on Arunai’s great mountain, will be freed from 
them,” said Brahma, repository of  the four Vedas, and began to relate 
a number of  stories.	 (611)

“At one time the eight Vasus had come to Our great assembly. 
Observing the way in which they boasted about their great austerities and 
fame, I declared, ‘It is a sin for you to speak boastfully in the company 
of  Agastya and the rest of  the rishis. It will render useless and annul all 
the austerities you have performed upon the earth.	 (612)

Those people who speak about the austerities they have performed, 
and of  the greatness of  their virtuous conduct, and those who speak 
scornfully of  others will be immersed in an ocean of  sorrow and remain 
there,’ I said. Trembling with fear the Vasus, who had displayed their 
lack of  wisdom, said, ‘Pray tell us when this [curse] will be lifted!’ to 
which I replied, ‘It is only by the grace of  Lord Siva that your suffering 
will be ended.’	 (613)

As soon as I had finished speaking all the Vasus left my presence and 
travelled to a place on the shores of  the great river Ganges. Meditating 
upon the Lord for ages beyond measure, they performed the most 
arduous austerities. Then, one day, He who shares his form with the 
Maiden who is like the valli creeper appeared before them mounted 
upon a young bull, and asked, ‘What boon is it that you desire?’ ‘Grant 
us your grace and rescue us from the deadly ocean that is the curse of  
lotus-dwelling Brahma,’ they replied.	 (614)

With a benign countenance that Cloud of  compassion, [Lord Siva] said 
to the Vasus, ‘If  you desire to be absolved of  your sin, there is a unique 
city called Sthaleswara1, glorious upon the earth, and in that holy place 
there is a mountain called Arunagiri. Go to that place and perform 
pradakshina of  the mountain, starting in the eastern quarter. Dwell in 
each of  its eight directions and perform tapas.	 (615)

Perform rare and noble tapas in Arunai, girt by rice fields upon which 
swans dwell. When you have done so, the Mountain of  fire will appear 



186 Arunachala Puranam

before you in the eight-faced form of  Astamurtti 2, and your abiding 
heinous sins will be absolved.’ So saying, the Lord, who grants to all who 
seek refuge in Him the boon of  dwelling in his own likeness, returned 
to silver Mount Kailash.	 (616)

In the manner described by the Lord, who in former times burned up the 
three asuras along with their aerial cities, all the Vasus went and performed 
arduous tapas in holy Arunai. Then did the Mountain of  fire appear in 
its eightfold form, and abide there for the good of  this world, and so the 
Vasus destroyed that curse, great as a mountain, which they had incurred 
through boasting about the greatness of  their austerities.	 (617)

Dwelling in all the eight directions around the mountain, they performed 
puja to Lord Siva in his eightfold form. As a result they obtained the 
Lord’s grace and regained the rank and station they had enjoyed before. 
Yet though the Lord who embodies the essence of  the Vedas stood 
there in his eightfold form for the purpose of  removing sin, Vishnu3 
himself  could not know his foot through the precepts of  the shastras, 
and to this day his head is still unknown to myself.	 (618)

Having seen how the five-faced Lord, father to the six-faced Murugan, 
appeared in his eight-faced form through the tapas performed by the 
eight Vasus, all the gods together sang hymns of  praise,” said Brahma, 
whose four faces bestow compassion, to the rishi Sanaka. Then he 
proceeded to recount the tale of  how Tilottama, the sound of  whose 
voice resembles the lute, came to that grace-bestowing mountain.       (619) 

“Once the King of  the gods [Indra], came to me, saying that everyone 
was performing the most arduous austerities with the intention of  
usurping his exalted station. Having asked me to do something [to 
prevent it], he took his leave. 	 (620)

After Indra had left, I combined the beauty of  the yaksa maidens, the 
damsels of  heaven, the maidens of  the lower worlds, the maidens of  the 
earth, girt by the fair ocean, and the mountain nymphs with breasts like 
Mount Meru, and fashioned the maiden Tillotama, just as the wasp, by 
some trick of  magic, destroys the body of  a grub and gives it the form 



The Expiation of  Sins 187

of  a wasp.4 [Through my desire for her] I lost my self  control and my 
reason deserted me.5	 (621 & 622)

Realising my state, in which I pined for her, deluded by the arrows of  
Kama, she was repelled by what was in my mind, knowing that I was 
her father. Evading my grasp, she hid from me, and I searched for her 
in the four directions.	 (623)

She fled in the form of  a female deer and I pursued her as a stag; she 
flew off  in the form of  a female parrot and I pursued her in the form 
of  a male parrot; just as she was despairing of  any means of  escape, she 
saw rearing up in the distance the lofty form of  Arunai’s Lord. Crying 
‘[You are my] refuge,’ she made her way there.	 (624)

‘Rider on the bull, [which was] Vishnu (transformed through) his 
power of  illusion! You who share your form with Her whose waist is 
[so slim as to be almost] non-existent! You whose form is scorching 
fire! You who dance in Holy Alangadu!6 Light of  the Vedas, whose 
meaning men study!’ she cried, as if  she were a baby girl, prattling into 
his divine ear. 	 (625)

The divine form of  the Lord in its entirety became an ear to listen to 
the plea of  her whose locks were adorned with sweet-scented flowers. 
In no time at all He who shares his form with the maiden Parvati split 
open that great mountain and appeared in the guise of  a hunter, [with 
Parvati as a huntress], and the four Vedas as his dogs.	 (626)

When He, whose left half is She whose fair arms are adorned by bangles, 
appeared brandishing a bow, my delusion and the [ensuing] suffering 
on account of Her whose (tiny) waist wilted (beneath her full breasts), 
disappeared. Thinking, ‘I shall incur the wrath of the Lord of the Dance,’ 
I was afraid and bowed down before Him. For when a bird sees a hunter, 
will it be afraid or will it not?	 (627)

‘The sin of desiring the maiden with breasts like mountains, whom you 
yourself created, could not be absolved in ten million kalpas. However 



188 Arunachala Puranam

you have experienced the sight of this supreme Mountain whose form 
is compassion, and consequently We have freed you from that sin. 
Go on your way!’ said the Lord of miracles, and retired back into the 
mountain.	 (628)

If  one who in the body has unintentionally brought evil karma upon 
himself  goes to southern Arunai, that karma will be dispelled; this much 
is certain. In the past there have been many who have dispelled their evil 
karma and gained the boons they sought in that place,” said Brahma, 
and related this story to rishi Sanaka:	 (629) 

“Once, at the end of  one kalpa, the darkness was receding and the light 
was returning once more as decreed by the Lord. There was only a little 
of  the period of  darkness left, yet Vishnu carried on sleeping in the 
much-lauded ocean, quite unaware of  this. Enlightened yogis fixed their 
thoughts upon Lord Siva.	 (630)

Lord Siva, the pure one, who cuts away the anava malam 7 of  his devotees, 
assumed the form of  a fiery radiance. In all places, in all directions, 
everywhere, he manifested as an infinite expanse, and from the sparks 
that showered down from Him, the whole world came into existence.
	 (631)

Those yogis went to the Imperishable One who had remained 
slumbering in the ocean, not realising that the darkness at the kalpa’s end 
had given way to light. As soon as they woke him up, saying, ‘Long may 
you prosper,’ Vishnu leapt up in terror from his [serpent] couch.	   (632)

‘Because I remained in deep slumber with the serpent (Adisheshan) as 
my couch, without creating lotus-dwelling Brahma to create the world, 
I have incurred evil karma through my base actions,’ he cried, then, 
with all five senses paralysed by fear, he turned his thoughts to my Lord 
[Siva].	 (633)

Since it is his flawless task to come swiftly and appear before his devotees 
the moment they think of  Him, and dispel their suffering, the Lord 
appeared, like a sun enthroned upon a mountain of  adamantine crystal, 



The Expiation of  Sins 189

mounted upon a white bull and accompanied by her whose form is like 
the flashing of  lightning bolts.	 (634)

Rising swiftly, he worshipped the feet of  the Lord and praised 
his compassionate nature. Having mollified Him through the 
performance of  puja accompanied by arghya 8 and the other acts of  
homage, he cried, ‘Pray grant me your grace, and wipe out the evil 
karma that clings to me!’ The Lord who wears [a garland of] erukka 
flowers, replied:	  (635)

‘Even if  you performed the horse sacrifice9 and the other sacrifices 
many times, the fetters of  your karma could not be removed. If  you 
wish to break the grip of  your karma, which brings with it a bondage 
that cannot be escaped, go and perform puja in Arunai’s city, which 
shines with brilliant light in the southern quarter. Then will your evil 
karma fall from you and depart,’ said the Lord.	 (636)

‘My own form and that city, girt by fragrant groves, are one and the 
same; they are not different; its glory even the Vedas cannot know. 
Yogis adorned with holy ash, ninety-six Brahmas, and yourself  (in) many 
(previous incarnations) have performed puja in that place.	 (637)

The bright Moon, sinking between the breasts of  Rohini10, adorned 
with fine jewellery, forgot the existence of  the other twenty-six sisters. 
Dakshan accordingly cursed him to dwindle [in power and beauty]. It 
was in that very place that the enfeebled Moon was released from his 
suffering.	 (638)

Go to that tall city which confers liberation upon those who merely 
think of  it and remove your mental affliction,’ the Lord concluded, 
and disappeared from view. Seeking the Mountain of  Fire, Hari set off  
in that direction and had sight of  Sonagiri, fragrant with the intense 
perfume of  the lotus blossoms upon its tanks.	 (639)

Seeing that mountain, which is the very form of  the Lord, and 
lovingly paying homage, he proceeded to the glorious holy city. With 
joy in his heart he bathed in the Indra tirtham, completed pradakshina, 



190 Arunachala Puranam

performed puja, and so, delivered from his evil karma, became free of  
all defilement.”	 (640)

Having told how the most exalted Vishnu went to Arunai’s city and 
attained the reward he sought, freeing himself  from sin, Brahma went 
on to recount [to Sanaka] how, on account of  a civet cat, the Lord of  
the Red Mountain, who caused Uma to dwell as one side of  himself, 
was much pleased.’	 (641)



References

1.	 See v. 327, note 35.

2.	 aṭṭa mūrtti, Sanskrit aṣṭa mūrti, the eight forms of Śiva. Their Tamil names are 
as follows: pūmi – earth, nīr – water, tēyu – fire, vāyu – air, ākāyam – ether, 
iyamāṉaṉ – the sacrificer, sūriyaṉ – the sun, cantiraṉ – the moon.

3.	 Here Viṣṇu is called Cītaraṉ, as the one who carries Lakṣmi on his breast.

4.	 There was a Hindu belief based on the phenomenon of a grub or caterpillar being 
transformed into a hornet or wasp. The analogy is known as bhramara-kīṭa-
nyāya, and states how the caterpillar gets transformed into a wasp by intensely 
thinking of the latter. Even so, the jīva becomes Brahman itself by meditating 
intensely on the latter. See also Ramana’s own account in Talks with Sri Ramana 
Maharshi, no. 592.

5.	 The Māhātmya gives a novel explanation of how the force of Brahmà’s desire 
caused him to have four faces. In that account Tilottama performs pradakṣiṇa 
of Brahmā as a stratagem for trying to escape his embrace:

	 asyāṁ pradakṣiṇāṁ bhaktyā kurvāṇāyāṁ prajāpateḥ, catasṛbhyo ’pi digbhyo 
’sya mukhāni udabhavan kṣaṇāt.

	 While she was devoutly circumambulating, there sprung up [four] faces of 
Brahmā in the four directions instantaneously.

	 Ar. Mā, Pūr. Ch. 5, sl. 53.

6.	 The temple of Tiruvālaṅkāṭu built by the Cholas during the 12th century, (though 
inscriptions evidence the 5th century) is regarded as a sacred Saivite temple 
in that it is one of the five majestic cosmic dance halls of Lord Śiva, known as 
ratna sabai. The other four sabais are Chidambaram, Madurai, Kutralam and 
Tirunelveli. Legend has it that when the Lord was once entranced in a deep 
cosmic dance, the jewels from the Lord’s anklets fell onto the earth and scattered 
in five places, Tiruvālaṅkāṭu being one of them.

7.	 See the note to v.148

8.	 arukkiyam, Sanskrit arghya is the offering of water. It is one of the 16 acts of 
homage and honour paid to deities and venerable personages. 

9.	 See v. 558 note 2.

10.	 Urōkiṇi, Sanskrit Rohini is a daughter of Dakṣa. Soma, the moon, was married to 
all 27 of Dakṣa’s daughters, but bestowed all his affection on only one, Rohini. 
For this Dakṣa cursed him to suffer from a wasting disease. The manner of his 
cure varies in different accounts, but in most cases provides the explanation for 
his periodic waxing and waning.






Chapter 13

புளகாதிபச் சருக்கம்

King Pulaka






“An asura by the name of  Pulaka, who possessed the ability to change 
himself  into many different forms, once roamed about on Arunagiri in 
the form of  a civet cat, shedding his precious civet there, thus winning 
[from the Lord] such great strength that the inhabitants of  the earth grew 
afraid. Pulaka was constantly performing the most heinous of  deeds, and 
so the gods and rishis all betook themselves to Mount Kailash, where 
He dwells who is like a mountain of  compassion, and who shares his 
form with Parvati, the daughter of  the Himalaya mountain, and spoke 
to Him in these terms:	 (642)

‘An asura called Pulaka, adopting the form of  a wild beast, has visited 
the seven worlds, one after the other, causing affliction, and everyone 
is suffering,’ they complained. The Flawless One addressed them thus, 
‘Since he has shed upon the mountain a civet whose fragrance knows 
no measure, he has acquired great strength. He will not easily be 
vanquished. Therefore We shall see to it that he ascends to the sweet 
state of  liberation, whilst you rise up out of  this sea of  troubles.’     (643)

Having bid them return, the Lord who is the source of  the Vedas called 
out, ‘Asura, you shall give up this life, in which you inhabit the form 
of  a civet cat!’ to which he who bore the civet cat’s form replied, ‘You 
who share your form with the maiden [Parvati]! Abode of  compassion! 
Ancient One! Supreme of  supremes! Three-eyed One, grant me your 
grace! Pray grant the request of  one who is ever ready to do your bidding.’ 
Thus he spoke, then fell at the Lord’s feet in worship.  (644)

‘In order that this civet cat race of  mine may be represented upon your 
holy body in the form of  fragrant civet, may you adopt as one of  your 
names, He whom civet adorns ! So saying he praised the Lord and left his 
bodily form, whereupon the Lord granted him his grace, so that the 
civet cat asura attained salvation.  Then, with melting hearts, rishis, gods 
and men adorned the Lord’s moon-crowned matted locks with civet. 
And so the temple of  the Lord ever runs with rivers of  civet [dissolved 
in] rosewater.	 (645)

Those who have given so much as an atom of  civet, rosewater or perfume 
for the Lord’s adornment, those who feasting their eyes upon Him, 



196 Arunachala Puranam

seeking his presence and praising his twin feet, have listened [to this 
history] will obtain liberation. For this is the history of  the Lord who 
bears the deer in his hand,” said lotus-borne Brahma. And Sanaka and 
the rest of  the rishis worshipped him, praising him with the blossoms 
of  their words.’	 (646)



வாழ்த்து
Praise

The holy Five Syllables, long may they prosper!  
Arunai, long may you prosper!
The Lord of  Arunai’s lotus feet, long may they prosper!  
His compassion, long may it prosper!
The heavens, with clouds that are like his compassion, 
long may they prosper!  
The earth, long may it prosper!   
The holy ash, which is our refuge upon the earth, 
long may it prosper!
The Saiva faith, long may it prosper!  Long may it prosper!	 (647)

Jnana, long may it prosper!  Virtue, long may it prosper!  
The land, the king, his wealth, his glory and his charity, 
long may they prosper!
Dharma, long may it prosper!
The truth of  the Vedas and Agamas, [long may it prosper!]
Music and learning, long may they prosper!
Those who cherish those who have acquired learning through study, 
long may they prosper!
The Lord’s holy feet, long may they prosper!  Long may they prosper!
	 (648)

Those who read, hear, write, or keep copies of  this history will be 
guardians of  the entire earth, surrounded by the dark ocean; they will 
live happily, flourishing in health and blessed with progeny, and through 
the grace of  Arunesan, our foundation and support, they will attain the 
state of  Sivam.	  (649)	
	






Glossary and Index
The main aims of  this Glossary and Index are to give the meanings of  
words which are not explained in the notes, and to give accurate tran-
scriptions, using diacritic marks, of  Tamil (and some Sanskrit) proper 
names and other words used in the text. At least one verse reference 
is given for each word, and considerably more for the more important 
characters, places and concepts. The correct Tamil transcription, using 
the scheme of  the University of  Madras Tamil Lexicon, is given in square 
brackets following each entry, followed sometimes by the Sanskrit (ab-
breviated as S.) version, where this differs significantly from the version 
in the text. For example: 

Sonachala [Cōṇācalam, S. Śoṇācala]
Gautama [Kautamar]

In the text, words are given in the way that most nearly represents their 
pronunciation in English without the use of  diacritics. This spelling de-
termines the alphabetical ordering of  the entries. The reasoning behind 
the above scheme is that it is important for the non-linguist not to be 
distracted by a mass of  confusing diacritic marks which often give little 
or no clue to pronunciation, whilst it is equally important for those who 
are able to access the text in the original Tamil to be able to correctly 
identify the Tamil and Sanskrit names and locate them in dictionaries. 
Numbered references are to the number of  the verse.

	 also in an unspecified number of following verses.

to destroy Dakṣa’s sacrifice; Vīraṉ, 170, 194, 



Glossary and Index 207

196, 200; eṅ(kaḷ) Aiyaṉ, 171,172,197; Entai – 
My father (as synonymous with Śiva) 198.

virasaiva [vīracaivar]	 Followers of a reformist branch of Saivism who 
abjure temple worship and carry a small lingam 
on their person. Often known as lingayats, and 
here referred to as jaṅgamar, 464.

Vishnu [Viṣṇu]	 Preserver of the universe; Uvaṇa neṭuñ koṭiyōṉ 
– as having the garuda as ensign 3; ñālam 
aḷippavaṉ – as preserver of the world 5; Viriñcaṉ 
tātai – as the father of Brahmā 17; Māyavaṉ, 
95; Mātavaṉ, 102; Nāraṇaṉ, 113, 261; Kāṉ-
k-kaṇṇaṉ – as flute-playing Kriṣṇa 121; Paṭi 
aḷantavaṉ – as measuring the earth in three 
strides 138; Cītaraṉ – as the bearer of Lakṣmi 
141, 296; Neṭiyavaṉ – as being very tall 144; 
Māyaṉ 196, 630; Māyōṉ, 437.

Vyasa [Viyācar, S. Vyāsa]	 Compiler of the Vedas; see v. 1, n. 1; 1,21, 515.
yaksha [iyakkaṉ]	 A class of celestial beings, 193, 622.
Yama [Iyamaṉ]	 The god of death and regent of the south. See 

v. 61, n. 48; Kālaṉ, 5, 264, 525; Antakaṉ – as 
the Destroyer 59, 151, 529; Kūṟṟaṉ, 240; 
Namaṉ 335.

yuga [yukam]	 An age of the world; each successive world 
goes through four of these. See v. 53 and note.

Online Resources

King Vallalan of  Tiruvannamalai
Translated by Marye Tonnaire and edited by David Godman.
http://davidgodman.org/asaints/kvallalan1.shtml
Contains a translation of  chapter 7, Vallala Maharaja, with detailed historical 
background and copious notes which could not be included in this edition.

Living in the embrace of Arunachala
Richard Clarke
http://richardarunachala.wordpress.com
Contains detailed photographic guides to many aspects of  the Tiruvannamalai sthala 
and the mountain Arunachala, including detailed accounts of  the inner and outer 
pradaskhina routes and the main Arunachaleswarar temple.
